# Product Requirements Document — AI Video Studio

**Version:** 2.1 | **Status:** Expanded product specification | **Baseline:** 2026-11-06
**Scope:** Browser-based professional non-linear video editor with AI-assisted workflows.

> This document defines requirements, not shipped functionality. The register contains 1,075 individually identified requirements across 25 product domains. A feature is implemented only after code, tests, and acceptance evidence exist.

## 1. Vision
Create a browser-first professional editing studio combining deterministic multitrack editing with optional AI assistance. Users import media, arrange a timeline, edit precisely, apply effects and motion, improve audio, caption content, preview, and render deliverables. AI proposals must be inspectable, previewable, permission-checked, and reversible.

## 2. Product principles
- Non-destructive editing; source files remain immutable.
- Deterministic timeline using integer frame ticks or rational/sample precision.
- Preview/export parity for every supported effect.
- AI is advisory by default; explicit approval precedes mutation.
- Private-by-default media and least-privilege access.
- Honest progress and capability states; no fake controls or success claims.
- Graceful degradation for unsupported codecs, browsers, providers, and effects.
- Asynchronous ingest, AI, and render tasks are observable, retryable, cancellable where safe, and resource-bounded.

## 3. Target users and jobs
- Educators: record, edit, annotate, caption, and publish lessons.
- Engineers: create walkthroughs, demos, bug reproductions, and release videos.
- Marketing teams: adapt edits into multiple aspect ratios and delivery formats.
- Customer-success teams: produce tutorials, onboarding videos, and explainers.
- Independent creators: combine precision manual editing with optional AI workflows.

## 4. Scope and priorities
- P0: workspace/project foundation, private media ingest, canonical timeline, core editing, preview, autosave/recovery, and real MP4 export.
- P1: annotations, transitions, keyframes, effects, audio mixing, captions, silence detection, cursor/tutorial effects, and creator presets.
- P2: AI planning/generation, collaboration, publishing, brand systems, billing, integrations, and platform-scale administration.
- P0 gate: import real media, create a sequence, edit a multitrack timeline, save/recover, render a downloadable file, and verify output. Tests must cover authorization, timeline invariants, persistence, upload recovery, and render failure recovery.

## 5. Core user journeys
### Manual edit
Authenticate → create workspace/project → import and validate files → create sequence → place clips → edit/inspect → preview → autosave → render → validate output → download.
### AI-assisted tutorial
Import screen recording → probe metadata → transcribe → request edit → generate typed plan → inspect affected ranges/confidence → preview → approve selected operations → apply as one undoable transaction → review → render.
### Review workflow
Invite collaborators → assign roles → share versioned review link → collect timecoded comments → resolve feedback → create revision → render approved output.

## 6. UX requirements
- Dark professional workspace with resizable tool/library, preview, inspector, and multitrack timeline.
- Desktop-first precision editing; responsive dashboard and review experience.
- Distinguish selection, playhead, range, hover, snapping, and active-tool states.
- Keyboard shortcuts, context menus, visible save state, focus management, and empty/loading/error/offline states.
- Timeline zoom, scrolling, track virtualization, and stable zoom anchoring.
- Identify proxy/low-quality preview and unsupported media/effect paths.
- Never present an unimplemented capability as an active control.

## 7. Requirement conventions
Each feature has a stable ID, domain-specific behavior, user outcome, acceptance condition, priority, and dependency. Acceptance requires reproducible automated tests or documented manual verification. Requirement IDs are canonical references for issues, pull requests, tests, and release notes.

## 8. Detailed feature register — 1,075 requirements

**Register:** 25 domains × 42 requirements = 1050 unique feature IDs.

### 8.1 Workspace and project lifecycle
**Scope:** Projects, workspaces, folders, ownership, settings, lifecycle, recovery, and organization.
**Priority:** P0 foundational release.

- **VS-0001 — Workspace and project lifecycle: create.** Create with validated defaults and a stable identifier. **User outcome:** complete this workflow without damaging source media or canonical project state. **Acceptance:** valid input succeeds; invalid input returns an actionable error; unauthorized access is denied; state persists after reload; failure leaves data consistent. **Priority/dependency:** P0; requires the domain model, server-side authorization, validation, and applicable shared services. ### 8.19 Collaboration and review

## 9. Non-functional requirements
- Reliability: asynchronous jobs use idempotency keys, bounded retries, dead-letter handling, and observable terminal states.
- Performance: timeline interactions avoid synchronous network/render work; large timelines use normalized state and virtualization.
- Integrity: project mutations are transactional, revisioned, validated, and recoverable; source assets remain immutable.
- Security: enforce tenant/resource authorization server-side; validate file signatures and probes; sandbox media processing; protect secrets and signed URLs.
- Privacy: document provider data flows, retention, deletion, model-training policies, and consent before cloud AI.
- Accessibility: keyboard operation, semantic labels, visible focus, contrast, captions, and reduced-motion support.
- Compatibility: publish a tested browser/codec matrix and graceful-degradation behavior.
- Observability: correlate requests and jobs with structured logs, metrics, traces, and audit events.

## 10. Success metrics
- Project creation-to-first-preview completion rate.
- Upload success and resumable-recovery rates; ingest latency.
- Timeline command latency at representative project sizes.
- Autosave success, recovery-point age, and recovery success.
- Render success, render-time ratio, retries, and output validation failures.
- AI plan acceptance, partial approval, undo rate, and correction rate.
- Caption word error rate and manual correction time on consented evaluation sets.
- Crash-free sessions, accessibility defects, and support incidents per active project.

## 11. Out of scope and constraints
- No claim of parity with proprietary desktop NLEs or use of proprietary UI assets.
- No guaranteed cursor reconstruction without pointer metadata; inferred movement must be labeled and reviewed.
- No unlimited storage, upload size, render duration, AI tokens, or concurrency.
- Real-time simultaneous timeline mutation is deferred until conflict semantics are proven.
- Native desktop and mobile precision editing are not first-release requirements.

## 12. Dependencies and unresolved decisions
- Resolve PHP runtime policy mismatch: composer.json declares PHP ^8.3 while AGENTS.md says PHP 8.5.
- Confirm frontend architecture and TypeScript build strategy before editor implementation.
- Select FFmpeg/FFprobe versions, worker isolation, storage strategy, proxy strategy, and resource limits.
- Define supported codecs/containers and publish a tested compatibility matrix.
- Define schema and retention for projects, sequences, tracks, clips, assets, revisions, jobs, and renders.
- Define AI provider consent, retention, data residency, budget controls, and evaluation gates.

## 13. Release acceptance gates
### Gate A — foundation
- Authentication, ownership, and authorization tests pass.
- Real upload, validation, probing, thumbnails, and retry/recovery pass.
- Project schema migrations, serialization, and version compatibility pass.
### Gate B — manual editor
- Timeline operations satisfy invariants and undo/redo round trips.
- Preview and inspector operate on canonical state.
- Autosave, offline/error states, and reload recovery are verified.
### Gate C — render
- Worker is isolated, resource-bounded, observable, and retryable.
- Output is probed and validated against requested settings.
- Failed/cancelled jobs preserve project state and provide actionable diagnostics.
### Gate D — advanced workflows
- Effects, captions, AI, collaboration, billing, and integrations ship only with security, accessibility, and operational acceptance tests.

## 14. Definition of done
A requirement is Implemented only when code exists in the correct layer, server-side validation and authorization are enforced, success and failure paths are tested, UI states are complete, relevant telemetry exists, documentation is updated, and status is reflected in CURRENT_STATE.md. A PRD entry alone is not implementation evidence.

## 15. Traceability
- UX: EDITOR_UX.md and UX_FLOWS_AND_STATES.md.
- Timeline: TIMELINE_ENGINE.md and PROJECT_FORMAT.md.
- Ingest: MEDIA_PIPELINE.md. Render: RENDERING.md.
- AI: AI_AUTOMATION.md, AI_PROVIDER_STRATEGY.md, AI_EVALUATION.md.
- Platform: ARCHITECTURE.md, DATA_MODEL.md, API.md, SECURITY.md, PERFORMANCE.md, DEPLOYMENT.md, OBSERVABILITY_RUNBOOKS.md.
- Quality/release: TESTING.md, TDD.md, RELEASE_AND_MIGRATION.md.
